| Ta Kung Pao, 18 Jan 1963, NPTKP19630118 leaf n3 (第四版), 本報專訪 「世代家園不能拆遷 九龍城砦老居民訴述處境」 | 陳拉's wife (surname 郭, given name hidden in the print) looked after nine children at home and also strung beads (穿珠仔) for a few ten-cent coins a day. | "在家除照顧九個兒女外，還「穿珠仔」，日賺幾毫，幫補家計" (at home besides caring for nine children she also 'strings beads', earning a few ten-cent pieces a day to help the family) |
| Ta Kung Pao, 18 Jan 1963, NPTKP19630118 leaf n3 (第四版), 本報專訪 「世代家園不能拆遷 九龍城砦老居民訴述處境」 | INFERRED: Their floor had been bought with money borrowed from relatives and friends, and the loan was not yet repaid. | "他住的這一層樓是向親友借錢買下的" (the floor he lives on was bought with money borrowed from relatives and friends) |

| Ta Kung Pao, 18 Jan 1963, NPTKP19630118 leaf n2 (第三版), 「城砦老居民反對拆遷」 (上接第四版), first columns | INFERRED: She lived at No. 7 社公街 (Sz Kung Street) in the City, on an upper floor. INFERRED reading: the print is faint; the floor is 二樓 or 三樓, and her name (it looks like 梁…) cannot be read. | Ta Kung Pao, 18 Jan 1963, NPTKP19630118 leaf n2 (第三版), 「城砦老居民反對拆遷」 (上接第四版) | INFERRED: She said that her house had been pulled down five times: once by the Japanese, once by fire, and three times by the Hong Kong government. Each time she rebuilt it with difficulty and did not leave the City. | "其餘三次係被香港政府有關方面的拆毀" (the other three times it was pulled down by the Hong Kong government) |
| Ta Kung Pao, 18 Jan 1963, NPTKP19630118 leaf n2 (第三版), 「城砦老居民反對拆遷」 (上接第四版) | INFERRED: She said that one year only three households were left in the City, hers among them. | "尤其是有一年城砦只走剩我三家人" (especially one year when only three households, mine among them, were left in the City) |

- **Leads:**
  - Other Ta Kung Pao issues of 1963 with City items, found by the finder's API query: 1963-01-14, -21, -22, 02-07, 05-31, 09-03 (archive.org NPTKP19630114 etc.).
  - Ta Kung Pao, 18 Jan 1963, leaf n2 (第三版), 「城砦老居民反對拆遷」: a shoe hawker (鞋販) with nine children, tenant at 社公街七號, and a woman of 66 at 龍津後街五號 who lived through two fires and a demolition order. Not yet extracted.
### china-4: British Encroachment on China's Sovereignty

- **Status:** Read on 2026-11-18; a checker checked a sample of the facts on 2026-11-20.
- **Citation:** 'British Encroachment on China's Sovereignty', Peking Review (北京周報), Vol. 6 No. 4, 25 Jan 1963, p. 20. https://www.marxists.org/subject/china/peking-review/1963/PR1963-04.pdf
- **Class:** primary (An English-language PRC weekly of the time, one week after the events.)
- **Date of the record:** 1963-01-25 (issue date)
- **Files:**
  - `china-4-PR1963-04-8ef89ae5.pdf` (in `local/`, not in git): Peking Review 1963 No. 4, whole issue, 24 scanned pages, no text layer (5,890,620 bytes); the City section is on p. 20; https://www.marxists.org/subject/china/peking-review/1963/PR1963-04.pdf; 5890620 bytes; SHA-256 `2468eb6ee25192b19fa5f5b92ed746070652330058aaa2b883716e5957a9efaa`; licence: no licence statement found on the host index page; 1963 periodical of the PRC government (https://www.marxists.org/subject/china/peking-review/index.htm)

| Page | Fact | Words in the source |
|---|---|---|
| Peking Review, Vol. 6 No. 4, 25 Jan 1963, p. 20 (PDF page 20), section 'British Encroachment on China's Sovereignty' | Peking Review said that the City is in the north-east part of Kowloon and that more than 20,000 people lived in it. | "The City of Kowloon is situated in the northeastern part of Kowloon and inhabitated by more than 20,000 people." |
| Peking Review, Vol. 6 No. 4, 25 Jan 1963, p. 20 (PDF page 20), section 'British Encroachment on China's Sovereignty' | On 7 Jan 1963 the Resettlement Department issued 'demolition and resettlement notifications' for about 200 houses. The English text gives the deadline as 20 Jan 1963 at the latest. The People's Daily of 18 Jan 1963 (china-2) gives deadlines from 8 to 20 Feb 1963: the two sources disagree. | "demolition of some 200 houses was to be completed by January 20 at the latest" |
| Peking Review, Vol. 6 No. 4, 25 Jan 1963, p. 20 (PDF page 20), section 'British Encroachment on China's Sovereignty' | The article said that home-owners and people with estate interests would lose heavily, and that thousands of people would be in a desperate plight. | "Home-owners and those with estate interests would sustain heavy losses and thousands would be in a desperate plight." |
| Peking Review, Vol. 6 No. 4, 25 Jan 1963, p. 20 (PDF page 20), section 'British Encroachment on China's Sovereignty' | The PRC Foreign Ministry lodged a strong protest with the British Government on 17 Jan 1963. | "The Chinese Foreign Ministry on January 17 lodged a strong protest with the British Government" |
| Peking Review, Vol. 6 No. 4, 25 Jan 1963, p. 20 (PDF page 20), section 'British Encroachment on China's Sovereignty' | On 19 Jan 1963 the 'Anti-Demolition and Anti-Resettlement Committee of the Residents of Kowloon' issued a statement against the decision. People's Daily (china-3) dates the committee's press conference to 18 Jan, with the Xinhua dispatch of 19 Jan. | "On January 19, the Anti-Demolition and Anti-Resettlement Committee of the Residents of Kowloon issued a statement" |
| Peking Review, Vol. 6 No. 4, 25 Jan 1963, p. 20 (PDF page 20), section 'British Encroachment on China's Sovereignty' | The article said that the authorities had tried several times in past years to clear the City and that the residents had stopped them. | "made repeated attempts to put their decision into effect but these were frustrated by the local residents" |

| Page | Fact | Words in the source |
|---|---|---|
| NPKS19361023 leaf n12 (本埠新聞), article 九龍城內鄉民搬遷案 | The residents inside Kowloon City had had repeated orders to move. The latest, the third notice, told them to leave by the end of 1936. | "最近為第三次之通告，限令於本年底遷出" |
| NPKS19361023 leaf n12 (本埠新聞), article 九龍城內鄉民搬遷案 | On 22 Oct 1936 a reporter went into the City and met the villagers' representative 楊偉雄. The New Territories South District Office (新界南約理民府) had put the deadline back to the end of the first lunar month of 1937, about two months more. | "展緩至來年舊曆正月底為止" |
| NPKS19361023 leaf n12 (本埠新聞), article 九龍城內鄉民搬遷案 | The villagers drafted a petition and sent a man to Canton to post it to the Foreign Ministry, asking the Foreign Minister (張外長) to take up the case. | "請求張外長負責交涉" |
| NPKS19361026 leaf n8, article 九龍城內居民搬遷案籲請外部澈底解決 | INFERRED: Several hundred residents petitioned the Foreign Ministry. On 25 Oct 1936 the reporter went into the City again; every household said it did not want to move. | "據各家戶之表示，皆不欲搬遷" |
| NPKS19361026 leaf n8, article 九龍城內居民搬遷案籲請外部澈底解決 | The petition said that the walled area was Chinese land, and that earlier talks by Guangdong and Guangxi diplomats had each got a satisfactory British answer. | "認為城內地域乃我領土" |
| NPKS19361101 leaf n8 (本埠新聞), article 港政府令九龍城居民遷徙 | The Foreign Ministry's commissioner for Guangdong and Guangxi, 刁作謙, had sent his secretary 凌士芬 to look into the matter. 凌士芬 first urged the residents to move, but they were attached to their old homes. | "凌氏始勸諭該處居民他遷，後以民情眷戀故土" |
| NPKS19361101 leaf n8 (本埠新聞), article 港政府令九龍城居民遷徙 | INFERRED: The residents petitioned the Foreign Ministry again on the 27th, which I take as 27 Oct 1936 (the article says 昨廿七日). | "昨廿七日又復呈外交部" |
| NPKS19361101 leaf n8 (本埠新聞), article 港政府令九龍城居民遷徙, text of the letter | 凌士芬 wrote to the residents that the case was now with the British Consul-General in Canton and the Foreign Ministry, and asked them to work in peace and wait. | "尚希望諸先生安心作業，靜候解決" |
| NPKS19361129 leaf n8, article 黃慕松刁作謙昨批覆九龍城居民 | The New Territories South District Office wanted to develop Kowloon City, ordered the residents to move, and chose a site at 沙田頭 for them. The paper gives the Chinese name only; I do not identify the place. | "擇定沙田頭地段，為該處居民搬遷之用" |
| NPKS19361129 leaf n8, article 黃慕松刁作謙昨批覆九龍城居民 | In Nov 1936 the City had 64 households and more than 430 residents. | "查該處現有住戶六十四家，居民四百卅餘人" |
| NPKS19361129 leaf n8, article 黃慕松刁作謙昨批覆九龍城居民 | The residents said they were born and raised there, and that their houses, though old and humble, were property left by their ancestors over centuries. | "屋宇雖殘陋，而為數百年來遺下之祖業" |
| NPKS19361129 leaf n8, article 黃慕松刁作謙昨批覆九龍城居民 | Representatives led by 楊偉雄 went to Canton to petition the Guangdong governor 黃慕松 and the commissioner 刁作謙. 刁作謙 took the case to the British Consul-General at Shameen and to the British Ambassador. | "晉謁廣東省府主席黃慕松" |
| NPKS19361129 leaf n8, article 黃慕松刁作謙昨批覆九龍城居民, text of 黃主席之批覆 | The governor's written reply, dated 20 Nov 1936, answered a petition of 16 Nov 1936 from the representatives. It said that the case was with the Foreign Ministry and the British, who were asked to make Hong Kong withdraw the order. | "廿五年十一月十六日請願書一件" |
| NPKS19361129 leaf n8, article 黃慕松刁作謙昨批覆九龍城居民, text of 特派員之批覆 (批廣字第四八零號) | INFERRED: The commissioner's reply names the petitioners as 楊偉雄, 陳德潮, 盧章 and 曾杜生. The third name is worn. | "批楊偉雄、陳德潮" |

- **Leads:**
  - The petition of the City residents (representative 楊偉雄 and others) to the Guangdong provincial government, 16 Nov 1936, and its reply of 20 Nov 1936 (批文)
  - The third removal notice of the New Territories South District Office (新界南約理民府) to the City residents, 1936, and the earlier two notices
  - 香港工商日報 of Oct 1936 before 23 Oct ("各情已見前報"), and the continuation of the 29 Nov 1936 article

- **Citation:** 香港工商日報 (Kung Sheung Daily News), Hong Kong, 31 January 1937, local-news page (本港新聞; scan leaf 10 of 19): "九龍城案久懸未決 城內住戶僅餘廿餘家 已呈外部請徹底解決" (no byline). Internet Archive item NPKS19370131 (uploaded 2023-12-25), https://archive.org/details/NPKS19370131
- **Class:** primary (A news report of the time, with the words of a residents' representative (reported speech). Background for the evictions of 1933-1937, long before the peak period.)

| Page | Fact | Words in the source |
|---|---|---|
| Kung Sheung Daily News (香港工商日報) 31 Jan 1937, scan leaf 10 (local news), article "九龍城案久懸未決 城內住戶僅餘廿餘家 已呈外部請徹底解決", cols. 1-2 | In January 1937 the case of the removal of the residents of the City was still not settled. The Chinese and British authorities were still studying what the treaty said. | "九龍城內居民遷徙案，久懸未決，中英當局尚在研究條約上所載各節" (The case of the removal of the residents inside Kowloon City has long hung unsettled; the Chinese and British authorities are still studying the clauses of the treaty.) |

| Kung Sheung Daily News (香港工商日報) 31 Jan 1937, scan leaf 10 (local news), article "九龍城案久懸未決 城內住戶僅餘廿餘家 已呈外部請徹底解決", cols. 3-4 | 羅章 said that the City once had more than sixty households, and that only about twenty-odd households were left in January 1937. | "以前共有住戶六十餘家，迄今僅餘廿餘家" (formerly there were more than sixty households in all; now only twenty-odd remain) |
| Kung Sheung Daily News (香港工商日報) 31 Jan 1937, scan leaf 10 (local news), article "九龍城案久懸未決 城內住戶僅餘廿餘家 已呈外部請徹底解決", cols. 4-5 | 羅章 said that, as far as he remembered, the Government owned six tenths of the property in the City. | "城內產業，官方佔十份之六" (of the property inside the City, the officials hold six tenths) |
| Kung Sheung Daily News (香港工商日報) 31 Jan 1937, scan leaf 10 (local news), article "九龍城案久懸未決 城內住戶僅餘廿餘家 已呈外部請徹底解決", cols. 5-7 | INFERRED: To get a final settlement, the remaining households had sent all the papers of the case to Nanjing the week before, to go to the Chinese Foreign Ministry. They asked it to negotiate the matter fully, and they were waiting for its answer. The word "final" (最後) is read from worn print. | "悉數寄往南京，轉呈中央外交部，請徹底交涉" (sent them all to Nanjing, to be passed on to the central Foreign Ministry, asking for thorough negotiations) |

- **Leads:**
  - Papers of the City residents sent to the Chinese Foreign Ministry in Nanjing in late January 1937 (the article refers to them); the Foreign Ministry files of the 1930s are in the Academia Historica (國史館) and the Institute of Modern History (近史所) archives.

- **Leads:**

| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 10 Dec 1947, scan leaf 3 (4th page, local news), article "九龍城居聯會三代表赴穗晉謁當局 我外交部不斷與英大使館接觸", Nanjing 特訊 | The Hong Kong Government had ordered the Kowloon City residents to move by the 11th (11 Dec 1947). | "關於香港政府令九龍居民於十一日遷移事" (concerning the Hong Kong Government's order that the Kowloon residents move on the 11th) |

| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 10 Dec 1947, scan leaf 3 (4th page, local news), article "九龍城居聯會三代表赴穗晉謁當局 我外交部不斷與英大使館接觸", Nanjing 特訊 | The source said that the formal papers on the case would be decided soon, because the date of the demolition was near. | "九龍城拆屋之期迫近，正式處理文件即可決定" (the date for pulling down the houses in Kowloon City is near; the formal papers can soon be decided) |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 10 Dec 1947, scan leaf 3 (4th page, local news), article "九龍城居聯會三代表赴穗晉謁當局 我外交部不斷與英大使館接觸", 路透社 Nanjing 9 Dec | The British Ambassador told Reuters in Nanjing on 9 Dec that the removal was an administrative matter of the Hong Kong Government for public health and safety. He said the place had no sanitary facilities and was often in danger of fire. | "因該處並無衛生設備，且時有火災危險云" (because the place has no sanitary facilities and is often in danger of fire) |

- **Leads:**
  - Hong Kong Public Works Department notice that ordered the residents of the City to leave by 11 Dec 1947 (the article refers to it).
  - Petition of the Kowloon City residents' association to the Guangdong provincial government, 9-10 Dec 1947.

Internet Archive item NPKS19471231 (uploaded 2023-12-25), https://archive.org/details/NPKS19471231
- **Class:** primary (A news report of the time (the day after the meeting of 30 Dec 1947). The words of the residents at the meeting are reported speech of witnesses. If not, officials would come and pull down the wooden huts in which they lived. | "飭令由昨（卅）日起從新向工務司及醫務署救濟部申請登記，否則派員督拆其所居木屋" (ordered them, from yesterday (the 30th), to apply anew for registration to the Public Works Department and the relief section of the Medical Department; otherwise officials would be sent to supervise the demolition of the wooden huts they lived in) |

One of the two men then went to the office to deny it, and said that another man had collected the rents in his name. This part of the print is worn; the reading is not certain. | "請予查究" (asks that it be investigated) |
| Kung Sheung Daily News (香港工商日報) 31 Dec 1947, page 5 (scan leaf 4), article "九龍城居民集會決定 任何威迫決不遷移 冒名收租請求查究 派員往外交部辦事處送遞呈文", section "業權問題", last block | At the meeting the residents all said that the land under the houses near the old wall base was public land: it had all been the wall base and the yamen (官衙) before. Thus no one could hold property rights over it, and the residents who paid had been misled. | "均爲公地，以前全爲城基及官衙" (all is public land; formerly it was all the wall base and the yamen) |
| Kung Sheung Daily News (香港工商日報) 31 Dec 1947, page 5 (scan leaf 4), article "九龍城居民集會決定 任何威迫決不遷移 冒名收租請求查究 派員往外交部辦事處送遞呈文", section "指証兩人曾來收租" | The residents said that the two men had let the land to residents who built their own houses on it, and collected a rent every month. The collection had started at the beginning of 1947. | "已由今年年初開始徵收" (the collection has gone on since the beginning of this year) |
| Kung Sheung Daily News (香港工商日報) 31 Dec 1947, page 5 (scan leaf 4), article "九龍城居民集會決定 任何威迫決不遷移 冒名收租請求查究 派員往外交部辦事處送遞呈文", section "指証兩人曾來收租" | INFERRED: Rents and key money in the City in 1947: for a rent of 30 dollars a month, the first month cost 80 dollars, of which 50 dollars was key money (鞋金). The paper does not name the currency; it is probably Hong Kong dollars (INFERRED). | "大約在每月三十元租者，最初之月須繳八十元，其中五十元爲鞋金" (roughly, for a rent of 30 dollars a month, 80 dollars had to be paid in the first month, of which 50 dollars was key money) |
| Kung Sheung Daily News (香港工商日報) 31 Dec 1947, page 5 (scan leaf 4), article "九龍城居民集會決定 任何威迫決不遷移 冒名收租請求查究 派員往外交部辦事處送遞呈文", section "指証兩人曾來收租" | For a rent of 50 dollars a month, the first month cost 140 dollars, of which 90 dollars was key money (鞋金). Other amounts varied. | "如月租五十元者，最初之月則收一百四十元，其中九十元爲鞋金" (for a rent of 50 dollars a month, 140 dollars was collected in the first month, of which 90 dollars was key money) |
| Kung Sheung Daily News (香港工商日報) 31 Dec 1947, page 5 (scan leaf 4), article "九龍城居民集會決定 任何威迫決不遷移 冒名收租請求查究 派員往外交部辦事處送遞呈文", section "指証兩人曾來收租", continued | INFERRED: The residents said that only these two men came each month to collect the rent. They showed rent tallies (租籌) as evidence; the tallies carried the signatures of the collectors. | "並有拿出收取租金之租籌，作爲證據" (and they brought out the rent tallies of the rent collected, as evidence) |
| Kung Sheung Daily News (香港工商日報) 31 Dec 1947, page 5 (scan leaf 4), article "九龍城居民集會決定 任何威迫決不遷移 冒名收租請求查究 派員往外交部辦事處送遞呈文", section "具呈外部請求查究" | The meeting agreed that every resident who had paid rent to the two men would sign at once. The residents' association would then send a written report to the Foreign Ministry office to tell the facts and ask for an inquiry. | "即席簽字署名，由居民聯會行文呈報外交部辦事處" (sign their names on the spot, and the residents' association would send a document reporting to the Foreign Ministry office) |
| Kung Sheung Daily News (香港工商日報) 31 Dec 1947, page 5 (scan leaf 4), article "九龍城居民集會決定 任何威迫決不遷移 冒名收租請求查究 派員往外交部辦事處送遞呈文", list of names at the end | INFERRED: The article ends with the names of the residents who signed, about forty people. The list is in worn print; I do not transcribe the names. | "等四十" (... and others, forty) |
| Kung Sheung Daily News (香港工商日報) 31 Dec 1947, page 5 (scan leaf 4), photo caption, left middle | INFERRED: Photo caption: the wall base of the City, a historic site called 「…亭」 (a pavilion; the first character is not clear), and the public land near them. The meeting accused 曾 and 謝 of letting the public land to other people to build houses. Photo by the paper's reporter 林…. | "及歷史遺跡" (and the historic site) |
| Kung Sheung Daily News (香港工商日報) 31 Dec 1947, page 5 (scan leaf 4), photo caption, top left | INFERRED: A second photo shows the residents' meeting of 30 Dec 1947, with men at a table. Its caption is printed white on black and is mostly not legible. | "九龍城居民" (Kowloon City residents) |

- **Leads:**
  - 華僑日報 (Wah Kiu Yat Po) 9 Jan 1948, Internet Archive NPWK19480109: headline "九龍城事件昨日我再向英使提嚴重交涉 監察院函外交部詳復 葉公超次長今日到港", and also "各方慰問龍城無家同胞", "褟叔烈並非失蹤曾生拒絕往警署", "老屋拆後種種", "聯會新蓋搭國旗高飄" (headline list of the item). This is the headline that plan.json gave for top-china-6; the repo does not hold that issue as an item.
  - Hong Kong Government notice of late Dec 1947 that ordered the City residents to register again with the Public Works Department and the relief section of the Medical Department from 30 Dec 1947.
  - Written report (呈文) of the Kowloon City residents' association to the Foreign Ministry office in Hong Kong, about 31 Dec 1947, on the two men who collected rent on public land, with about forty signatures.
  - 華僑日報 (Wah Kiu Yat Po) 31 Dec 1947, Internet Archive NPWK19471231: headline "龍城拆屋通告一束交特派員辦事處".
### china-24: 論「九龍事件」 (with 血濺九龍城, in 《新聲》 1948年第11期)

- **Status:** Read on 2026-11-18; a checker checked a sample of the facts on 2026-11-20.
- **Citation:** 招志堅〔阮仲琦〕, 「血濺九龍城（香港通訊）」, 《新聲》 1948年第11期, pp. 13-14, dated 20 Jan 1948; and 翟光〔劉家良〕, 「論『九龍事件』」, ibid., pp. 2-4, dated 22 Jan 1948. Chinese Marxists Internet Archive: https://www.marxists.org/chinese/reference-books/new-voice/3-1948-11-09.htm and https://www.marxists.org/chinese/reference-books/new-voice/3-1948-11-03.htm
- **Class:** primary (A Hong Kong correspondent's report sent on 20 Jan 1948, a week after the clash, and a commentary of 22 Jan 1948. Records made at the time. Not an official source: a Trotskyist periodical (托派多数派刊物), hostile to both the ROC government and the Hong Kong left. Its paragraphs on the City's past are background (kind secondary).)
- **Date of the record:** 1948-01-20 (report sent from Hong Kong); 1948-01-22 (commentary)
- **Files:**
  - `china-24-3-1948-11-09.htm-4c636719.html` (in `local/`, not in git): HTML transcription of 「血濺九龍城（香港通訊）」 (GBK-encoded page); https://www.marxists.org/chinese/reference-books/new-voice/3-1948-11-09.htm; 10902 bytes; SHA-256 `986a068c261cd6c6eaa900aeca85f643461d47275c9b6661384f029b1a84e8a4`; licence: no licence statement found for this transcription; 1948 periodical (https://www.marxists.org/chinese/reference-books/new-voice/index.htm)
  - `china-24-3-1948-11-03.htm-0ec87597.html` (in `local/`, not in git): HTML transcription of 「論『九龍事件』」 (GBK-encoded page); https://www.marxists.org/chinese/reference-books/new-voice/3-1948-11-03.htm; 11116 bytes; SHA-256 `e6b7ba08666de5a760999c68f5a2ee28cc35014908cf31a2350fcefe85d51488`; licence: no licence statement found for this transcription; 1948 periodical (https://www.marxists.org/chinese/reference-books/new-voice/index.htm)
- **Note:** its 2 secondary facts (narration, later captions) are in the secondary twin, [`../../secondary-sources/china-official-records-1933-1987/SOURCE.md`](../../secondary-sources/china-official-records-1933-1987/SOURCE.md).

| Page | Fact | Words in the source |
|---|---|---|
| 《新聲》1948年第11期, 招志堅〔阮仲琦〕「血濺九龍城（香港通訊）」, pp. 13-14 (marxists.org transcription 3-1948-11-09.htm) | The report called Kowloon City 'a tiny place of only 3,000 residents', next to Kai Tak airport on one side and Kowloon Tong on the other. | "九龙城，这一仅有三千居民的弹丸之地，一面靠近启德飞机场，另一面则邻近全港最华丽的住宅区：九龙塘" (Kowloon City, a tiny place of only three thousand residents, next to Kai Tak airport on one side and the finest residential area of Hong Kong, Kowloon Tong, on the other) |
| 《新聲》1948年第11期, 招志堅〔阮仲琦〕「血濺九龍城（香港通訊）」, pp. 13-14 (marxists.org transcription 3-1948-11-09.htm) | The report said that the government issued three removal orders in 1947; the last was dated 27 Nov 1947. It also dates a 'second removal order' to 29 Dec. The residents formed a residents' representatives' council to resist. | "十二月廿九日发出了第二次迁出令。居民坚决如故" (On 29 Dec the second removal order was issued. The residents stayed firm.) |
| 《新聲》1948年第11期, 招志堅〔阮仲琦〕「血濺九龍城（香港通訊）」, pp. 13-14 (marxists.org transcription 3-1948-11-09.htm) | On 5 Jan 1948 the government sent 36 police vans with 250 fully armed police with gas masks. More than 200 Public Works Department workers pulled down more than half of the huts within a few hours, and two residents' representatives were arrested. | "动员三十六辆冲锋车，装载着全副武装及防毒面具的军警二百五十名" (mobilised thirty-six riot vans carrying 250 fully armed police with gas masks) |
| 《新聲》1948年第11期, 招志堅〔阮仲琦〕「血濺九龍城（香港通訊）」, pp. 13-14 (marxists.org transcription 3-1948-11-09.htm) | The residents used stones and bricks against the police on 5 Jan 1948. | "手无寸铁的贫民，以石头砖瓦作武器，反抗魔手" (the unarmed poor used stones, bricks and tiles as weapons) |
| 《新聲》1948年第11期, 招志堅〔阮仲琦〕「血濺九龍城（香港通訊）」, pp. 13-14 (marxists.org transcription 3-1948-11-09.htm) | After the demolition the residents came down from the hillside and built temporary shelters on the ruins with the wood from the pulled-down huts. The ROC flag was raised over the ruins each morning. | "利用被拆除的木器在废墟上重建他们的临时居址以御风雨" (used the wood from the demolished huts to rebuild temporary shelters on the ruins against wind and rain) |
| 《新聲》1948年第11期, 招志堅〔阮仲琦〕「血濺九龍城（香港通訊）」, pp. 13-14 (marxists.org transcription 3-1948-11-09.htm) | On 12 Jan 1948 police gathered at the police station 紅磡差館: 10 police vans, several radio cars, 3 ambulances, and more than 100 armed police with rattan shields, batons, tear gas and guns. | "停有十辆冲锋车，数辆附有无线电之警车，三辆十字车" (ten riot vans, several police cars with radios, and three ambulances were parked) |
| 《新聲》1948年第11期, 招志堅〔阮仲琦〕「血濺九龍城（香港通訊）」, pp. 13-14 (marxists.org transcription 3-1948-11-09.htm) | The armed police appeared at the East Gate (東門口) of the City and went toward the temporary office of the residents' association, to pull down the remaining wooden huts. The report gives two times for this (11:30 am and 'about 8'), thus the hour is not sure. | "武装人员即在九龙城东门口出现，直向居民会临时办事处进发" (armed men appeared at the East Gate of Kowloon City and headed straight for the residents' association's temporary office) |
| 《新聲》1948年第11期, 招志堅〔阮仲琦〕「血濺九龍城（香港通訊）」, pp. 13-14 (marxists.org transcription 3-1948-11-09.htm) | The police first took cover behind a small triangular wooden hut at the East Gate, then charged with shields and batons. An officer gave the order to fire. | "警察一度以东门口之三角小木屋做掩蔽" (the police for a time sheltered behind the small triangular wooden hut at the East Gate) |
| 《新聲》1948年第11期, 招志堅〔阮仲琦〕「血濺九龍城（香港通訊）」, pp. 13-14 (marxists.org transcription 3-1948-11-09.htm) | A front line of some dozens of residents, led by 劉子良 and 張忠武, faced the police; about 300 women and children carried stones to them from behind. | "以刘子良张忠武等数十人构成的第一线任前锋，其余妇女小孩约三百人则在后方搬运石块" (a front line of some dozens led by 劉子良 and 張忠武; about three hundred women and children carried stones at the rear) |
| 《新聲》1948年第11期, 招志堅〔阮仲琦〕「血濺九龍城（香港通訊）」, pp. 13-14 (marxists.org transcription 3-1948-11-09.htm) | Tear gas covered the City. The residents retreated, with old people, children, clothes and quilts, toward South Wall Road (城南道). Five people were hurt: three men and two women. The police then razed the City. | "向城南道退却" (retreated toward South Wall Road) |
| 《新聲》1948年第11期, 招志堅〔阮仲琦〕「血濺九龍城（香港通訊）」, pp. 13-14 (marxists.org transcription 3-1948-11-09.htm) | After the clash, the police told the City residents by word of mouth to leave within one week, or be put on trains and expelled. The limit ended on 18 Jan 1948, and the day passed quietly. | "限他们在一星期内迁出，违令者将强迫押上火车驱逐出境" (ordered them to move out within a week; anyone who disobeyed would be forced onto a train and expelled) |
| 《新聲》1948年第11期, 招志堅〔阮仲琦〕「血濺九龍城（香港通訊）」, pp. 13-14 (marxists.org transcription 3-1948-11-09.htm) | Before 18 Jan 1948 the residents shared out all the relief goods, and their representatives told them to stay ready all night. | "九龙城居民预先分配了全部救济品，代表警告他们澈夜准备应变" (City residents shared out all relief goods in advance; representatives warned them to be ready all night) |
| 《新聲》1948年第11期, 招志堅〔阮仲琦〕「血濺九龍城（香港通訊）」, pp. 13-14 (marxists.org transcription 3-1948-11-09.htm) | The report said that on 17 Dec 1947 the Kowloon magistrate found residents guilty of occupying public land; commissioner 郭德華 then said that China did not want the small place to harm Sino-British relations, and his secretary 區樵 told residents to pull down their own huts. | "先行自己拆除，免于究治" (demolish [the huts] yourselves first, to avoid prosecution) |
| 《新聲》1948年第11期, 招志堅〔阮仲琦〕「血濺九龍城（香港通訊）」, pp. 13-14 (marxists.org transcription 3-1948-11-09.htm) | The report said that the Hong Kong left-wing paper 華商報 reported the City affair lightly and did not show sympathy for the residents. | "华商报没有一句话是表同情于被迫迁的居民的" (Hua Shang Bao had not one word of sympathy for the evicted residents) |
| 《新聲》1948年第11期, 翟光〔劉家良〕「論『九龍事件』」, pp. 2-4 (marxists.org transcription 3-1948-11-03.htm) | The commentary said that the protest movement in China after the 'Kowloon incident' was spontaneous, and that in Guangzhou it led to the 'Shameen incident' (沙面事件). | "这次“九龙事件”所引起的斗争是许多冲突积聚而成的，是无可抑制的，自发的" (the struggle that this 'Kowloon incident' caused was built up from many conflicts; it was irrepressible and spontaneous) |

- **Leads:**
  - 華商報, Hong Kong, Jan 1948: its one editorial on the Kowloon City incident, and its later editorial 'expressing sympathy' a week after the clash (cited).
  - 《新聲》 scans: the index page links a compiled PDF, https://www.marxists.org/chinese/reference-books/new-voice/new-voice.pdf (size not checked; the issue 11 pages would show the printed text).
### top-china-18: 九龍城事件外部再交涉 (華僑日報 1948-01-06)

- **Status:** Read on 2026-11-18; a checker checked a sample of the facts on 2026-11-20.
- **Citation:** 華僑日報 (Wah Kiu Yat Po), Hong Kong, 6 January 1948 (中華民國卅七年一月六日), local-news page (leaf 3 of the scan): lead article (特訊) "出動武警二百五十人苦力六七十人 龍城四十八屋一日蕩平", with photos by the paper's reporter; and 中央社 Nanjing dispatch of 5 Jan, "九龍城事件外部再交涉". Internet Archive item NPWK19480106, uploaded 2023-12-17, https://archive.org/details/NPWK19480106
- **Class:** primary (News reports of the time (the day after the event), with photos by the paper's own reporter. The words of the old resident (曾) and of 朱's wife are reported speech of witnesses. The report is not neutral: the paper sympathises with the residents. Background for the 1948 evictions, not for the peak period.)
- **Date of the record:** 1948-01-06 (events of 5 Jan 1948)
- **Files:**
  - `top-china-18-n3-70440bba.jpg` (in `local/`, not in git): Page image of Wah Kiu Yat Po 6 Jan 1948, local-news page (leaf n3), with the lead report and photos of the demolitions of 5 Jan 1948 and the 中央社 item on the Foreign Ministry; https://archive.org/download/NPWK19480106/page/n3.jpg; 5549873 bytes; SHA-256 `0a500e25c4e36968d3d1b338e035c2d5e03bc13aecb82cc13897cff1ad202243`; licence: No licence statement; archive.org metadata has no rights or licenseurl field; copyright of the newspaper (https://archive.org/details/NPWK19480106)

| Page | Fact | Words in the source |
|---|---|---|
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 6 Jan 1948, scan leaf 3 (4th page, local news), lead article "龍城四十八屋一日蕩平", para. 1 | On 5 January 1948 the Hong Kong police and the Public Works Department (工務局) started to pull down the wooden huts inside the walled City. The paper calls it a decisive action without precedent. | "本港警察當局與工務局已於昨（五）日採取一空前未有之决定性行動" (The Hong Kong police authorities and the Public Works Department yesterday (the 5th) took an unprecedented decisive action.) |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 6 Jan 1948, scan leaf 3 (4th page, local news), lead article "龍城四十八屋一日蕩平", para. 1 | INFERRED: The demolition carried out an order that a magistrate of the Kowloon Magistracy (九龍裁判署) had given some days before. | "執行日前九龍裁判署法官" (carrying out [the order of] the Kowloon magistrate some days before) |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 6 Jan 1948, scan leaf 3 (4th page, local news), lead article "龍城四十八屋一日蕩平", para. 1 | The police sent more than 250 armed men. The off-duty police and detectives of all the police stations of Kowloon took part. | "此次警方出動武裝警員達二百五十名以上" (This time the police sent out more than 250 armed constables.) |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 6 Jan 1948, scan leaf 3 (4th page, local news), lead article "龍城四十八屋一日蕩平", para. 1-2 | INFERRED: The police carried helmets, gas masks, rattan shields, batons, and long and short guns. The paper says it was the largest police operation in Hong Kong since the war. The word for helmet is worn; it reads as 鋼盔 (steel helmet). | "警員等均配有鋼盔，防毒面具，籐牌警棍及長短鎗等" (The constables all had steel helmets, gas masks, rattan shields, batons and long and short guns.) |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 6 Jan 1948, scan leaf 3 (4th page, local news), lead article "龍城四十八屋一日蕩平", para. 2 | The Public Works Department hired more than 60 coolies to pull down the huts. The headline gives 60 to 70 coolies. | "工務局亦雇用苦力達六十餘名之多，前往拆屋" (The Public Works Department also hired as many as sixty-odd coolies to go and pull down the houses.) |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 6 Jan 1948, scan leaf 3 (4th page, local news), lead article "龍城四十八屋一日蕩平", para. 2 | The reporter reached Kowloon City Police Station (九龍城警署) at 5 a.m. The police had all got up at 4.30 a.m. and were assembling. Trucks borrowed beforehand stood at the station door. | "署內警員已全體於四時半起床" (The constables in the station had all got up at half past four.) |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 6 Jan 1948, scan leaf 3 (4th page, local news), lead article "龍城四十八屋一日蕩平", para. 2 | INFERRED: Senior officers came to the station before 6 a.m.: the acting Commissioner of Police 史派路, the superintendent 希翰 of Kowloon West (九龍西界), a Kowloon City superintendent (莫…), and inspector 衛仲賢. Two ambulances of Kowloon Hospital (九龍醫院) also came. | "代警務處長史派路" (acting Commissioner of Police 史派路) |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 6 Jan 1948, scan leaf 3 (4th page, local news), lead article "龍城四十八屋一日蕩平", para. 2-3 | At 6.50 a.m. the police column moved out, then the coolies, then the officers' cars. They went along Boundary Street (界限街) and South Wall Road (城南道) and lined up on Carpenter Road (賈炳達道) at 6.55. | "由界限街前進，然後轉入城南道" (went forward from Boundary Street, then turned into South Wall Road) |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 6 Jan 1948, scan leaf 3 (4th page, local news), lead article "龍城四十八屋一日蕩平", para. 3 | INFERRED: 朱沛唐 and 劉毅夫, representatives of the residents' association of Kowloon City of Bao'an County (寶安縣九龍城居民聯合會), shouted to the residents to rise up. The name of the association is partly worn. | "朱沛唐及劉毅夫正高呼居民起來" (朱沛唐 and 劉毅夫 were shouting "Residents, rise up!") |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 6 Jan 1948, scan leaf 3 (4th page, local news), lead article "龍城四十八屋一日蕩平", para. 3 | 劉毅夫 bent down to pick up a stone. Two police inspectors held his arms and arrested him at about 7.03 a.m. | "劉俯身拾石" (Liu bent down to pick up a stone.) |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 6 Jan 1948, scan leaf 3 (4th page, local news), lead article "龍城四十八屋一日蕩平", para. 3 | In the paper's account, 朱 put his right hand on a short gun at his left side. Inspector 衛仲賢 hit his hand with a baton. The police handcuffed 朱 and took him to Kowloon City Police Station at 7.05 a.m. | "朱猝伸出右手，按着左腹之短鎗" (Chu suddenly put out his right hand and pressed it on the short gun at his left side.) |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 6 Jan 1948, scan leaf 3 (4th page, local news), lead article "龍城四十八屋一日蕩平", para. 4 | At 7.20 a.m. the Public Works foremen and the coolies went into the huts, climbed into the lofts and kicked the board walls down. Thus the huts had lofts (閣) and walls of boards. | "登閣上，以足猛蹴板壁，板壁應聲" (they climbed into the lofts and kicked hard at the board walls, which [fell] at once) |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 6 Jan 1948, scan leaf 3 (4th page, local news), lead article "龍城四十八屋一日蕩平", para. 4 | The coolies first stood still and did not know what to do. They started work only when the foremen urged them. | "均一時呆住不" (all stood stunned for a time) |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 6 Jan 1948, scan leaf 3 (4th page, local news), lead article "龍城四十八屋一日蕩平", para. 4 | By 7.39 a.m. the people of the nearby huts began to take down their own huts. The police ring closed round the whole hut area. | "附近之木屋紛紛自動拆卸" (the nearby wooden huts were one after another taken down by their owners) |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 6 Jan 1948, scan leaf 3 (4th page, local news), lead article "龍城四十八屋一日蕩平", para. 4 | The only injury on the day: the coolie 程善, aged 47, was hit by falling timber on the right hand, face and left leg. He stayed in Kowloon Hospital. | "僅有苦力程善（四十七歲）" (only the coolie 程善 (47 years old)) |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 6 Jan 1948, scan leaf 3 (4th page, local news), lead article "龍城四十八屋一日蕩平", para. 4 | The police had tear gas and ambulances ready. | "如催淚彈，隨行之救傷車等" (such as tear-gas bombs, and the ambulances that went with them) |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 6 Jan 1948, scan leaf 3 (4th page, local news), lead article "龍城四十八屋一日蕩平", para. 5 | A woman who worked as a stone-breaker (打石婦人) had hurt her foot some days before while breaking stone. She lay groaning when the huts came down, and an ambulance took her to Kowloon Hospital. This shows stone-breaking as women's work in the City in 1948. | "有一打石婦人，於日前打石被壓傷足，正在呻吟" (there was a stone-breaking woman, whose foot had been crushed while breaking stone some days before, groaning) |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 6 Jan 1948, scan leaf 3 (4th page, local news), lead article "龍城四十八屋一日蕩平", para. 5 | INFERRED: At 1 p.m. the police changed shifts. At 5 p.m. the police and the Public Works men left. About twenty-odd huts were levelled, and only fir poles lay piled in disorder. The work went on the next morning, 6 January. The headline gives 48 huts pulled down in one day. | "只餘杉木雜亂堆積而已。今晨仍繼續拆卸" (only fir timber was left piled in disorder. This morning the demolition continues.) |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 6 Jan 1948, scan leaf 3 (4th page, local news), lead article "龍城四十八屋一日蕩平", para. 6 | A police spokesman said that the two arrested representatives would be charged. The two were moved to Yau Ma Tei Police Station (油麻地警署) at noon. | "午解押油麻地警署" (at noon were taken under guard to Yau Ma Tei Police Station) |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 6 Jan 1948, scan leaf 3 (4th page, local news), lead article "龍城四十八屋一日蕩平", section "我官方之態度" | INFERRED: After the event, the representatives 陳振洲 and 劉子良 went to the office of the Chinese Special Commissioner (特派員公署辦事處) to ask him to take the matter up. The subheading prints the second name as 劉子… with a worn last character. | "振洲劉子良兩代表往特" (the two representatives [陳]振洲 and 劉子良 went to the Special [Commissioner's office]) |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 6 Jan 1948, scan leaf 3 (4th page, local news), lead article "龍城四十八屋一日蕩平", subheading | The residents' association sent three more men to petition the Bao'an County Government (寶安縣政府). | "另派三人向寶安縣政府請願" (and sent three more people to petition the Bao'an County Government) |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 6 Jan 1948, scan leaf 3 (4th page, local news), lead article "龍城四十八屋一日蕩平", subheading | The evicted residents slept in the open on the night of 5 January 1948. | "居民昨夜露宿" (the residents slept in the open last night) |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 6 Jan 1948, scan leaf 3 (4th page, local news), lead article "龍城四十八屋一日蕩平", section "居民方面態度" | The residents said that the timber they kept was worth little, that they had to stop their trades and pull down their own huts, and that they had nowhere to sleep. | "且須停業自拆，無形中要受雙重損失，晚上住宿亦成問題云" (and they must stop business and pull down [the huts] themselves, a double loss in effect; lodging at night is also a problem) |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 6 Jan 1948, scan leaf 3 (4th page, local news), section after "居民方面態度" | An old resident, 曾, said that houses in the City had been pulled down before: in the 5th year of the Republic (1916), then in the 12th, 19th and 22nd years (1923, 1930, 1933). He called 1948 the fifth time. The years are converted from the Republican calendar. | "九龍城民居遷拆事，遠在民國五年已發生一次，其後在民十二、十九、廿二均曾發生，此次爲第五次矣" (the demolition of the homes of the Kowloon City residents happened as far back as Republic year 5; later it happened in years 12, 19 and 22; this is the fifth time) |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 6 Jan 1948, scan leaf 3 (4th page, local news), section after "居民方面態度" | In 1933 曾 went with more than ten elders from his home district to the provincial capital to petition. He says that in the end they won. | "彼曾偕同鄉父老十餘人往省" (he went with more than ten elders of his home district to the provincial capital) |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 6 Jan 1948, scan leaf 3 (4th page, local news), section after "居民方面態度" | INFERRED: 曾 took the reporter to a second-floor home (二樓) on a street whose name starts 衙前 (the next character is worn). There the reporter met 朱沛棠's wife, née 李. The surname of the arrested man is printed 朱沛唐 elsewhere on the page. | "某號二樓會晤朱沛棠之妻李氏" (at No. so-and-so, second floor, met 李, the wife of 朱沛棠) |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 6 Jan 1948, scan leaf 3 (4th page, local news), section after "居民方面態度" | 朱's wife said that he left home very early that morning, and she did not know how he was arrested. She had a baby girl two months before. | "彼於二月前生一女" (she had given birth to a girl two months before) |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 6 Jan 1948, scan leaf 3 (4th page, local news), photo caption, centre right | Photo caption: armed police in full equipment surround the huts and watch over the demolition. | "裝備齊全之武警正包圍各屋督拆中" (Fully equipped armed police surround the houses and supervise the demolition.) |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 6 Jan 1948, scan leaf 3 (4th page, local news), photo caption, top left | Photo caption: a little girl, in tears, carries back a broken board; the caption asks where she can go. | "小姑娘含淚扛回破板向何處去？" (A little girl, in tears, carries back a broken board: where can she go?) |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 6 Jan 1948, scan leaf 3 (4th page, local news), article "九龍城事件外部再交涉", para. 1 | A Central News Agency (中央社) dispatch from Nanjing of 5 January: an AP report of 4 January from Hong Kong said that Foreign Minister 王世杰 called the eviction of the City residents an internal matter of the Hong Kong Government. | "關于九龍城居民之被逐，係香港政府內部問題" (the eviction of the Kowloon City residents is an internal problem of the Hong Kong Government) |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 6 Jan 1948, scan leaf 3 (4th page, local news), article "九龍城事件外部再交涉", para. 1 | INFERRED: The acting director of the Information Department (代理情報司長) of the Foreign Ministry, 時昭瀛, said that Minister 王 never said this, and that the Ministry had taken the case up with the British again and again. His title is in worn print. | "關於此案，外部曾向英國當局一再交涉，以期獲得圓滿之解決" (On this case the Foreign Ministry has repeatedly made representations to the British authorities, hoping for a satisfactory settlement.) |

- **Leads:**
  - Kowloon Magistracy (九龍裁判署) demolition order on the wooden huts in the City, late Dec 1947 or early Jan 1948 (the article refers to it; court record not in the repo).
  - Hong Kong Police and Public Works Department records of the operation of 5 Jan 1948 (named officers: acting Commissioner 史派路, superintendent 希翰, inspector 衛仲賢).
  - Associated Press dispatch from Hong Kong, 4 Jan 1948, that attributes the "internal problem" remark to 王世杰.
  - Kowloon Hospital (九龍醫院) admissions of 5 Jan 1948 (the coolie 程善 and a stone-breaking woman).
### top-china-5: 香港工商日報 1948-01-10

- **Status:** Partly read on 2026-11-18; a checker checked a sample of the facts on 2026-11-20. An earlier run of this chunk fetched the full-size image of leaf n4 on 2026-11-18. On 2026-11-20 I read the City items of leaf n4 (第五頁) from cropped, enlarged images: the commissioner's account and five-point protest, the Vice Minister's stop in Hong Kong, the relief article and the court article. I did not read the front-page item 「九龍城拆屋事件 王外長報告國府」, and I did not find the headline 九龍城同胞要米不須贈菓餅 that the plan names (it is not in the item's description). Checker (2026-11-20): I checked all 12 facts against the page image; five quotes were not exact and are now corrected (facts 1, 5, 6, 7, 8, 10).
- **Citation:** Kung Sheung Daily News (工商日報), Hong Kong, 10 Jan 1948, p. 5: 「九龍城拆屋事件交涉經過發表 郭特派員提出五點抗議 港督答覆態度完全背馳」; 「外次葉公超過港 居民報告被拆屋經過」; 「各團體熱烈響應慰問九龍城居民 收到現款糧食甚多」; 「九龍城被拘兩代表昨再提堂」. Internet Archive item NPKS19480110, https://archive.org/details/NPKS19480110
- **Class:** primary (A Hong Kong newspaper of the time, four days after the demolition of 5 Jan 1948.)
- **Date of the record:** 1948-01-10

  - `top-china-5-NPKS19480110-12d9791c.pdf` (in `local/`, not in git): Kung Sheung Daily News, 10 Jan 1948, whole issue, page-image PDF (4,683,450 bytes); City items on leaf n4 (p. 5) and the front page; https://archive.org/download/NPKS19480110/NPKS19480110.pdf; 4683450 bytes; SHA-256 `be31ee1caf98931e1df1cf099411daeb8c3982d0bf2b2f892c7b7545486dea74`; licence: no rights statement on the item (https://archive.org/details/NPKS19480110)

|---|---|---|
| Kung Sheung Daily News (工商日報), 10 Jan 1948, archive.org NPKS19480110, leaf n4 (第五頁) | Commissioner 郭德華 told the Central News Agency on 9 Jan 1948 that he had met the Governor four times: on 29 Nov, 2 Dec, 18 Dec and 23 Dec 1947. | "計第一次爲去年十一月廿九日，第二次爲十二月二日，第三次爲同月十八日，第四日爲同月廿三日" (the first time was 29 Nov last year, the second 2 Dec, the third the 18th of that month, the fourth the 23rd) |
| Kung Sheung Daily News (工商日報), 10 Jan 1948, archive.org NPKS19480110, leaf n4 (第五頁) | 郭德華 based China's position on the 1898 Convention for the Extension of Hong Kong Territory (中英展拓香港界址專條): China never gave up jurisdiction over Kowloon City. | "依據一八九八年中英展拓香港界址專條，中國政府對其在九龍城之管轄權從未放棄" (under the 1898 Sino-British Convention for the Extension of Hong Kong Territory, the Chinese government never gave up jurisdiction in Kowloon City) |
| Kung Sheung Daily News (工商日報), 10 Jan 1948, archive.org NPKS19480110, leaf n4 (第五頁) | INFERRED: He said that on 5 Jan 1948 the Hong Kong government, without notice and while talks went on, pulled down the houses in the City by force. | "乃香港政府竟於本年元月五日決[定]，突然捨棄外交途徑，且事前並不通知，實行武力拆毀該城內住屋" (yet on 5 January this year the Hong Kong government suddenly abandoned diplomacy and, without notice, demolished the houses in the City by force) |
| Kung Sheung Daily News (工商日報), 10 Jan 1948, archive.org NPKS19480110, leaf n4 (第五頁) | On 9 Jan 1948 he met the Governor a fifth time and made a five-point protest: the British side bears the blame; the evicted residents must be rehoused first; the arrested residents 朱沛唐 and 劉毅夫 must be freed; the destroyed houses must be rebuilt; China does not accept any British breach of the treaty. | "被捕居民朱沛唐、劉毅夫二人，應即立即釋放" (the arrested residents 朱沛唐 and 劉毅夫 must be released immediately) |
| Kung Sheung Daily News (工商日報), 10 Jan 1948, archive.org NPKS19480110, leaf n4 (第五頁) | He said that the Governor's answer was completely opposite to the Chinese view. | "港督答覆態度完全背馳" (the Governor's reply was completely contrary) |
| Kung Sheung Daily News (工商日報), 10 Jan 1948, archive.org NPKS19480110, leaf n4 (第五頁) | On 9 Jan 1948 groups, schools and private people brought money, goods and food to the residents whose houses had been pulled down, from morning to night. | "昨日各界團體、學校、及個人携備欵物糧食，到九龍城慰問房屋被拆，流離失所之居民" (yesterday groups, schools and individuals brought money, goods and food to Kowloon City to comfort the homeless residents) |
| Kung Sheung Daily News (工商日報), 10 Jan 1948, archive.org NPKS19480110, leaf n4 (第五頁) | The residents' association shared the food out by head: each person got five 盅 of white rice (about 3 catties), three bread rolls and a few biscuits; those with a baby also got a tin of condensed milk. | "如有嬰孩者，另發給煉乳一罐" (those with a baby are also given a tin of condensed milk) |
| Kung Sheung Daily News (工商日報), 10 Jan 1948, archive.org NPKS19480110, leaf n4 (第五頁) | A charity (慈善社) sent 50 cotton blankets. The association gave them out only after night rounds, to those who slept in the open and had no blanket. | "至慈善社送來棉毡五十張，則俟夜間巡視露宿者情況，確實至無被毡禦寒者，始行發給" (as for the fifty cotton blankets sent by the charity, they were given out only after night rounds of those sleeping in the open showed who truly had no blanket against the cold) |
| Kung Sheung Daily News (工商日報), 10 Jan 1948, archive.org NPKS19480110, leaf n4 (第五頁) | INFERRED: The association gave the food only to residents who truly had nowhere to live and still slept in broken houses. INFERRED reading: 處 in the quote is blotted. | "規定確屬無處棲依，目下仍在破屋露宿者，始行發給" (it was laid down that only those who truly had nowhere to shelter and still slept in broken houses would be given it) |
| Kung Sheung Daily News (工商日報), 10 Jan 1948, archive.org NPKS19480110, leaf n4 (第五頁) | Vice Foreign Minister 葉公超 passed through Hong Kong on 9 Jan 1948 on his way back from Burma. City residents' representatives met him at the airport and told him of the demolition. | "適當九龍城居民被拆事件發生" (it happened just when the demolition of the Kowloon City residents' houses took place) |
| Kung Sheung Daily News (工商日報), 10 Jan 1948, archive.org NPKS19480110, leaf n4 (第五頁) | INFERRED: 葉公超 told the representatives that they could hire a lawyer for the two arrested representatives. He told reporters that his stop was not about the City, and that a treaty question was for international law. INFERRED reading: 解 is blotted, and the words before the quote (如係關於條約上問…) are partly unclear. | "則須由國際法[解]決" ([if it is a treaty question,] then it must be settled by international law) |
| Kung Sheung Daily News (工商日報), 10 Jan 1948, archive.org NPKS19480110, leaf n4 (第五頁) | The chairman of the City residents' association, 朱沛唐, and its representative 劉毅夫 were held for protesting the demolition. They came before the court again on 9 Jan 1948 and were remanded to the next Monday. | "九龍城居民聯會主席朱沛唐，代表劉毅夫兩人" (Kowloon City residents' association chairman 朱沛唐 and representative 劉毅夫) |

- **Leads:**
  - Five-point protest of commissioner 郭德華 to the Governor, 9 Jan 1948 (quoted).
  - Kowloon Magistracy proceedings against 朱沛唐 and 劉毅夫, Jan 1948 (reported).

### top-china-17: 社評：廣州的不幸事件 (新聞報 1948-01-17, 第二版)

- **Status:** Read on 2026-11-20; a checker checked a sample of the facts on 2026-11-20.
- **Citation:** 新聞報 (Xinwenbao), Shanghai (上海新聞報報社), 17 January 1948 (中華民國三十七年一月十七日), page 2: 社評 "廣州的不幸事件". Internet Archive item xinwenbao-shanghai-1948.01.17 (collection xinwenbao-shanghai, uploaded 2021-11-19), https://archive.org/details/xinwenbao-shanghai-1948.01.17/page/n1
- **Class:** primary (An editorial of the time in a leading Shanghai daily. It is opinion, and it reports events of the day before. It gives the reaction in China, not facts on the City. Background for 1948, not for the peak period.)
- **Date of the record:** 1948-01-17 (events of 16 Jan 1948)
- **Files:**
  - `top-china-17-n1-0de98fc0.jpg` (in `local/`, not in git): Page image of the Xinwenbao (Shanghai) 17 Jan 1948, page 2 (leaf n1), with the editorial on the Canton riot that followed the Kowloon City affair; https://archive.org/download/xinwenbao-shanghai-1948.01.17/page/n1.jpg; 922033 bytes; SHA-256 `40e14e18d2950f6d1e22f4916ffb71e80adca93359ee1ebba0f5041c86b669b0`; licence: No licence statement; the archive.org item has no rights or licenseurl field; the uploader gives no rights (https://archive.org/details/xinwenbao-shanghai-1948.01.17)

|---|---|---|

| Xinwenbao (新聞報), Shanghai, 17 Jan 1948, page 2 (scan leaf n1), editorial (社評) "廣州的不幸事件", para. 1 | On the afternoon of 16 Jan 1948 a few people left a march in Canton that protested the Kowloon City affair. They burned the British Consulate and wrecked the British Information Office. | "焚燬英國領事館，並搗毀英國新聞處" (burned down the British Consulate and smashed the British Information Office) |
| Xinwenbao (新聞報), Shanghai, 17 Jan 1948, page 2 (scan leaf n1), editorial (社評) "廣州的不幸事件", para. 1 | INFERRED: The day before the riot, the Canton committee of all circles to support the Kowloon City cause (廣州各界九龍城事件後援會) met to plan the route of the march. The provincial chairman 宋子文 tried hard to keep the march away from June 23 Road (六二三路, named for the Shakee massacre of 23 June 1925) and from Shameen, where the British Consulate stood, to avoid trouble. | "廣州各界九龍城事件後援會開會，討論遊行路線" (the Kowloon City Affair Support Association of all circles in Canton met to discuss the route of the march) |
| Xinwenbao (新聞報), Shanghai, 17 Jan 1948, page 2 (scan leaf n1), editorial (社評) "廣州的不幸事件", para. 3 | The editorial says that, after the war, the Hong Kong Government used force against the residents of Kowloon City in an imperialist way, and that this angered the Chinese people. | "藉武力對付九龍城的居民" (used armed force against the residents of Kowloon City) |
| Xinwenbao (新聞報), Shanghai, 17 Jan 1948, page 2 (scan leaf n1), editorial (社評) "廣州的不幸事件", last para. | The editorial reports that Foreign Minister 王世杰 had met the British Ambassador 施諦文 late into the night, and that the British Ambassador had passed the Chinese demands to London. | "外長王世杰與英大使施諦文曾於深夜作懇切商談" (Foreign Minister 王世杰 and British Ambassador 施諦文 held earnest talks late at night) |
### top-china-15: 抗議九龍城事件 寶安學生臥軌 暹華僑對此亦表憤慨 (中央日報 1948-01-18, 第二版; with 英殖民部發表聲明 仍為港府措施狡辯 and 吳鐵城談話)

- **Status:** Partly read on 2026-11-20; a checker checked a sample of the facts on 2026-11-20. I read page 2 (BookReader leaf n1) at half scale (3052x4166 px) with enlarged crops. I read all of the article on the Bao'an students and the Chinese in Siam, the London dispatch on the Colonial Office statement, and the opening of the 吳鐵城 talk. CHECK of the finder: the 吳鐵城 talk is about the riot at Shameen in Canton after the Kowloon City affair; it urges a diplomatic settlement and condemns the rioters; I did not transcribe all of it, because it has no facts on the City. I did not read the item on the Premier (張院長) and Shameen. The OCR text is garbled; I read the images. CHECK 2026-11-20: the London item is a 中央社 dispatch (中央社倫敦十六日專電), not a 本報 dispatch; the quotes of all 8 facts were corrected against the page image.
- **Citation:** 中央日報 (Central Daily News), 18 January 1948, page 2: "抗議九龍城事件 寶安學生臥軌 暹華僑對此亦表憤慨" (中央社 Bangkok, Canton and Nanjing dispatches of 17 Jan); "英殖民部發表聲明 仍爲港府措施狡辯 謂拆除陋屋係因妨礙公共衛生" (中央社倫敦十六日專電); "吳鐵城談話" (中央社南京十七日電); "港府派機撤退穗英僑" (中央社廣州十七日電). Internet Archive item zhongyang-ribao-1948.01.18, https://archive.org/details/zhongyang-ribao-1948.01.18/page/n1
- **Class:** primary (News dispatches of the time in the party paper of the Kuomintang (國民黨). The Colonial Office statement is reported at second hand, in the words of the government agency 中央社 (London, 16 Jan), under the paper's hostile headline. The report that the Bao'an students lay on the rails is a rumour (外傳), and the railway bureau denies it in the same dispatch. Background for 1948, not for the peak period.)
- **Date of the record:** 1948-01-18 (events of 16-17 Jan 1948)
- **Files:**
  - `top-china-15-n1-ca4bfa52.jpg` (in `local/`, not in git): Page image of the Central Daily News 18 Jan 1948, page 2 (leaf n1), with the reports on the rumoured Bao'an student protest, the Colonial Office statement and the talk of 吳鐵城; https://archive.org/download/zhongyang-ribao-1948.01.18/page/n1.jpg; 7505081 bytes; SHA-256 `789cbedd099c428193850693406048443cfd18de51ee27c3fbbca24373825a7d`; licence: No licence statement; the archive.org item has no rights or licenseurl field; the uploader gives no rights (https://archive.org/details/zhongyang-ribao-1948.01.18)

|---|---|---|
| Central Daily News (中央日報) 18 Jan 1948, page 2 (第二版; scan leaf n1), article "英殖民部發表聲明 仍爲港府措施狡辯 謂拆除陋屋係因妨礙公共衛生" (中央社倫敦十六日專電), para. 1 | The Colonial Office in London made a statement on 16 Jan 1948 about the demonstration in Canton. In the words of the agency 中央社, it sought to defend the sentence of the Hong Kong Government on two Chinese residents: three months of hard labour for "resisting the demolition of Chinese houses in the old City of Kowloon". | "企圖爲香港政府判處「抗拒拆毀九龍舊城中國民房」之二中國居民苦役三個月之事辯護" (sought to defend the sentence of the Hong Kong Government of three months of hard labour on two Chinese residents for "resisting the demolition of Chinese houses in the old City of Kowloon") |
| Central Daily News (中央日報) 18 Jan 1948, page 2 (第二版; scan leaf n1), article "英殖民部發表聲明 仍爲港府措施狡辯 謂拆除陋屋係因妨礙公共衛生" (中央社倫敦十六日專電), para. 1 | The Colonial Office said that the Chinese houses were a serious danger to health, and that this was the reason for their removal. It said that the matter had been exaggerated and distorted, and had started all kinds of rumours. | "因中國民房對衛生有嚴重之妨礙、故必須予以拆除" (because the Chinese houses were a serious hindrance to health, they had to be removed) |
| Central Daily News (中央日報) 18 Jan 1948, page 2 (第二版; scan leaf n1), article "英殖民部發表聲明 仍爲港府措施狡辯 謂拆除陋屋係因妨礙公共衛生" (中央社倫敦十六日專電), para. 1 | The Colonial Office said that, in fact, this small number of Chinese with no right to live there were turned out because their huts had no proper sanitary facilities. It said that the huts were built of timber and could easily start fires. | "此批少數無權居住之中國居民、係因其陋屋並無適當之衛生設備、故予以驅逐" (this small number of Chinese residents with no right of residence were turned out because their huts had no proper sanitary facilities) |
| Central Daily News (中央日報) 18 Jan 1948, page 2 (第二版; scan leaf n1), article "英殖民部發表聲明 仍爲港府措施狡辯 謂拆除陋屋係因妨礙公共衛生" (中央社倫敦十六日專電), para. 1 | The Colonial Office said that the huts were not pulled down before the Hong Kong Government had offered the residents other houses in their place. It said that in Britain itself any public-health authority would have taken the same action. | "在香港政府允以代替之房屋給予上述居民之前、此批陋屋並未拆除" (these huts were not pulled down before the Hong Kong Government agreed to give the said residents houses in their place) |
| Central Daily News (中央日報) 18 Jan 1948, page 2 (第二版; scan leaf n1), article "抗議九龍城事件 寶安學生臥軌 暹華僑對此亦表憤慨", 中央社廣州十七日電 (opening, and the railway bureau statement after it) | A 中央社 dispatch from Canton of 17 Jan reported a rumour (外傳) that on 16 Jan students of Bao'an county (寶安), to protest the Kowloon City affair, gathered where the Chinese and British sections of the Kowloon-Canton Railway meet and lay on the rails to stop the trains going into the British section. The same dispatch then gave the word of the railway bureau: all the trains of the British section reached Kowloon safely on 16 Jan (十六日英段各次列車、均安全到達九龍). Thus the protest on the rails is not confirmed, although the headline of the paper gives it as fact. | "外傳寶安學生、爲抗議九龍城事件、於十六日集結廣九鐵路中英段交界處、實行臥軌" (it is rumoured that the Bao'an students, to protest the Kowloon City affair, gathered on the 16th at the junction of the Chinese and British sections of the Kowloon-Canton Railway and lay on the rails) |
| Central Daily News (中央日報) 18 Jan 1948, page 2 (第二版; scan leaf n1), article "抗議九龍城事件 寶安學生臥軌 暹華僑對此亦表憤慨", 中央社曼谷十七日專電 | The Chinese newspapers in Bangkok (Siam) shared the anger of the people in China at the measures of the Hong Kong Government in Kowloon. An editorial of 中原報 said that the Chinese in Siam would fully support the Chinese government in negotiating with Britain a reasonable settlement of the Kowloon affair and of the question of the return of Hong Kong to China. | "香港政府在九龍之措施極表憤慨" (expressed great anger at the measures of the Hong Kong Government in Kowloon) |
| Central Daily News (中央日報) 18 Jan 1948, page 2 (第二版; scan leaf n1), article "港府派機撤退穗英僑" (中央社廣州十七日電) | After the riot in Canton, a special aircraft of the airline 香港航空公司, sent by the Hong Kong Government, landed at Baiyun airport (白雲機場) at 7 a.m. on 17 Jan 1948. It took out 18 British residents (11 women, 6 children and 1 man) and left for Hong Kong at 8 a.m. | "港政府派來撤退在穗婦孺之香港航空公司專機" (the special aircraft of 香港航空公司 that the Hong Kong Government sent to take out the women and children in Canton) |
| Central Daily News (中央日報) 18 Jan 1948, page 2 (第二版; scan leaf n1), article "吳鐵城談話 督促政府以外交方式處置 並不容奸宄份子乘機搗亂" (中央社 Nanjing 17 Jan), opening | A 中央社 dispatch from Nanjing of 17 Jan says that, after the Hong Kong Government forced the City residents out again and again and caused the unfortunate events, people all over China protested in good order. But in Canton rioters joined the march and set fire to the British Consulate-General at Shameen. The reporter then asked 吳鐵城, secretary-general of the party headquarters (中央黨部秘書長), for his view; the headline says that he urged a diplomatic settlement and condemned the troublemakers. | "致有縱火焚燒沙面英國總領事館事件" (so that there was the burning of the British Consulate-General at Shameen) |

- **Leads:**
  - Colonial Office statement of 16 Jan 1948 on the Kowloon City demolitions (UK National Archives, CO 537 or FO 371 files; the English text would give the British account of the huts).
  - Hong Kong court sentence of about mid-January 1948 on the two residents' representatives (three months of hard labour for resisting the demolition).
### top-china-7: 華僑日報 1948-01-29

- **Status:** Read on 2026-11-18; a checker checked a sample of the facts on 2026-11-20.
- **Citation:** 華僑日報 (Wah Kiu Yat Po), Hong Kong, 29 January 1948 (中華民國卅七年一月廿九日). Leaf 1 (local news): "龍城居民呈外特處 三日內不釋朱劉 願全體入獄受苦" and "梁創出院追述留醫取彈經過" (特訊, signed 冰); "省府振米今晨分派" and "菲島粵僑慰問龍城" (特訊, signed 冰). Leaf 2: "外部鄭重否認我已同意賠償沙面事件損失" with "九龍城事件未獲英方復文不考慮沙面事件解决辦法" (中央社, 合衆社 and 路透社 dispatches of 27-28 Jan). Internet Archive item NPWK19480129, https://archive.org/details/NPWK19480129
- **Class:** primary (News reports of the time. The words of 梁創 and of the residents' petition are first-hand (reported speech); the statements of the Foreign Ministry and the summary of the British note are official words reported by news agencies. The paper sympathises with the residents. Background for 1948, not for the peak period.)
- **Date of the record:** 1948-01-29 (events of 26-28 Jan 1948)

It said they had asked the Foreign Ministry to recover the sovereignty of Kowloon and raised money. | "卅七年元月廿四日" (24 January, Republic year 37) |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 29 Jan 1948, scan leaf 2 (3rd page, China news), block "外部鄭重否認我已同意賠償沙面事件損失" / "九龍城事件未獲英方復文不考慮沙面事件解决辦法", lead para. | The Foreign Ministry spokesman denied that China had agreed to pay for the British losses at Shameen. An authoritative source said China would not consider a Shameen settlement before Britain answered on the Kowloon City affair. | "我方於獲得英方對九龍城事件之覆文前，不能考慮沙面事件之具體解決辦法" (before we get the British reply on the Kowloon City affair, we cannot consider a concrete settlement of the Shameen affair) |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 29 Jan 1948, scan leaf 2 (3rd page, China news), block "外部鄭重否認我已同意賠償沙面事件損失" / "九龍城事件未獲英方復文不考慮沙面事件解决辦法", 中央社 Nanjing 28 Jan, Q&A | On 28 Jan, 時昭瀛, Director of the Information Department (情報司長) of the Foreign Ministry, said that the British note of 26 Jan to Ambassador 鄭天錫 set out the British view of the Kowloon City affair. China had not yet answered it. | "外部情報司長時昭瀛" (時昭瀛, Director of the Information Department of the Foreign Ministry) |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 29 Jan 1948, scan leaf 2 (3rd page, China news), block "外部鄭重否認我已同意賠償沙面事件損失" / "九龍城事件未獲英方復文不考慮沙面事件解决辦法", 合衆社 Nanjing 28 Jan | A United Press dispatch said Foreign Minister 王 told the Executive Yuan that the Kowloon City and Canton affairs would soon have a "peaceful" and "reasonable" settlement. | "日內將可獲得「和平」而「合理」的解決" (within days a "peaceful" and "reasonable" settlement can be reached) |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 29 Jan 1948, scan leaf 2 (3rd page, China news), block "外部鄭重否認我已同意賠償沙面事件損失" / "九龍城事件未獲英方復文不考慮沙面事件解决辦法", 路透社 London 27 Jan | Reuters summarised the British note: it reviewed the removal of the residents of 54 wooden huts in Kowloon City and the clashes when the Hong Kong police evicted people again on 5 and 12 January. | "遷移九龍城五十四間木屋之居民" (the removal of the residents of fifty-four wooden huts in Kowloon City) |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 29 Jan 1948, scan leaf 2 (3rd page, China news), block "外部鄭重否認我已同意賠償沙面事件損失" / "九龍城事件未獲英方復文不考慮沙面事件解决辦法", 路透社 London 27 Jan | INFERRED: The British note said that it would be "most wrong" for the Hong Kong Government to take no judicial action in the City only because the question of jurisdiction was under discussion. | "則係「極錯誤」者" (would be "most wrong") |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 29 Jan 1948, scan leaf 2 (3rd page, China news), block "外部鄭重否認我已同意賠償沙面事件損失" / "九龍城事件未獲英方復文不考慮沙面事件解决辦法", 路透社 London 27 Jan | The British note said the Bao'an county magistrate (寶安縣長) encouraged the trouble: after the first eviction he spoke to the City residents under the Chinese flag. | "曾在中國國旗之下向九龍城居民演說" (spoke to the Kowloon City residents under the Chinese national flag) |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 29 Jan 1948, scan leaf 2 (3rd page, China news), block "外部鄭重否認我已同意賠償沙面事件損失" / "九龍城事件未獲英方復文不考慮沙面事件解决辦法", 路透社 London 27 Jan | China's answer to the British claim for the burnt Canton consulate did not mention that claim, but asked for compensation for the losses of the Kowloon City residents. | "但又要求賠償九龍城居民之損失" (but asked for compensation for the losses of the Kowloon City residents) |
| Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報) 29 Jan 1948, scan leaf 2 (3rd page, China news), block "外部鄭重否認我已同意賠償沙面事件損失" / "九龍城事件未獲英方復文不考慮沙面事件解决辦法", 中央社 Nanjing 28 Jan, second item | 時昭瀛 said that before 5 Jan the Foreign Ministry and its Hong Kong representative warned the Hong Kong Government not to use force in an area where China had never given up jurisdiction. He blamed the shooting and tear gas of 12 Jan on Hong Kong's failure to heed this. | "在我國從未放棄治權之地域不得採取强制措施" (must not take coercive measures in an area where our country has never given up jurisdiction) |

- **Leads:**
  - British Foreign Office note of 26 Jan 1948 to the Chinese Ambassador 鄭天錫 on the Kowloon City affair (summarised by Reuters, London 27 Jan); probably in UK National Archives FO 371 files.
  - Chinese Foreign Ministry protest of 8 Jan 1948 to Britain (named in the same block: 一月八日之抗議).
  - Petition (呈文) of the Kowloon City residents' association to the Special Commissioner for Foreign Affairs in Hong Kong, 28 Jan 1948.
  - Residents' registration cards (居民登記證) of the Kowloon City residents' association, Jan 1948, and its relief accounts to 26 Jan 1948.
### top-china-21: 九龍城沙面兩事件交涉癥結仍為主權問題 (華僑日報 1948-01-31)

- **Status:** Partly read on 2026-11-20; a checker checked a sample of the facts on 2026-11-20. I looked at small images of all 8 leaves, then read leaves n0 and n3 at full size with enlarged crops. On n0 I read the opening of the lead (the three points of the deadlock); the rest of it is a general discussion of the treaty and the Kowloon lease, which I did not read. On n3 I read the delegates' petition, the 西鄉 relief item, and most of the long text of the British Foreign Office note that the Government Information Services released on 30 Jan 1948. CHECK of the finder: the note is the biggest City item on the page and is not in the finder's note. Some columns of the note are worn; I mark those facts inferred.
- **Citation:** Wah Kiu Yat Po (華僑日報), Hong Kong, 31 Jan 1948, front page (leaf n0) and local news page (leaf n3); scan on the Internet Archive, item NPWK19480131, https://archive.org/details/NPWK19480131, read 2026-11-20.
- **Class:** primary (A Hong Kong daily newspaper of the day. The British note that it prints is an official record of Jan 1948. The tree primary is correct.)
- **Date of the record:** 31 Jan 1948 (reports of 28-30 Jan 1948)
- **Files:**
  - `top-china-21-n0-a928c742.jpg` (in `local/`, not in git): Page image of Wah Kiu Yat Po 31 Jan 1948, front page, with the lead on the deadlock of the talks; https://archive.org/download/NPWK19480131/page/n0.jpg; 5274250 bytes; SHA-256 `aa4e272de547939288b3d65c80b8d1c391e766491fd484aa0c3e50924b806bf3`; licence: No licence statement; the archive.org item has no rights or licenseurl field; copyright of the newspaper is not stated (https://archive.org/details/NPWK19480131)
  - `top-china-21-n3-b3bbb21f.jpg` (in `local/`, not in git): Page image of Wah Kiu Yat Po 31 Jan 1948, local news, with the full Chinese text of the British Foreign Office note, the delegates' petition and the 西鄉 relief; https://archive.org/download/NPWK19480131/page/n3.jpg; 5415128 bytes; SHA-256 `98e8bc6d6ee6ed063562a7ff1e8ee4173d72e7f3f93db1ea3767fba883c80663`; licence: No licence statement; the archive.org item has no rights or licenseurl field; copyright of the newspaper is not stated (https://archive.org/details/NPWK19480131)
- **Note:** its 2 secondary facts (narration, later captions) are in the secondary twin, [`../../secondary-sources/china-official-records-1933-1987/SOURCE.md`](../../secondary-sources/china-official-records-1933-1987/SOURCE.md).

| Page | Fact | Words in the source |
|---|---|---|
| NPWK19480131 leaf n0 (front page), lead 九龍城沙面兩事件交涉癥結仍為主權問題 | On 29 Jan 1948 the talks had not moved. A British Embassy spokesman said the deadlock was on three points: jurisdiction over Kowloon City, the release of the arrested residents, and compensation. | "(一)九龍城之管轄權問題，(二)被捕居民釋放問題，(三)賠償問題" |
| NPWK19480131 leaf n3 (local news page), article 龍城代表請答覆釋放朱劉兩要求 | On 28 Jan 1948 the residents association sent delegates with a letter to the special commissioner 郭德華, asking him to press hard for the release of 朱沛唐 and 劉毅夫. If there was no answer in three days, the residents would all go to prison with them. | "三日內無答覆，則居民願全體入獄，共同受苦" |
| NPWK19480131 leaf n3 (local news page), article 香港政府新聞處發表英外部致中國大使覆牒全文 | The Government Information Services released, on the night of 30 Jan 1948, the full text of the British Foreign Office reply to the Chinese Ambassador on the Kowloon City affair. | "英外部致中國大使覆牒全文" |
| NPWK19480131 leaf n3 (local news page), British note | INFERRED: The note says that after the British reoccupation in 1945 people squatted on the site and built huts with no proper sanitation, a fire and disease risk. This was the government's reason for the clearance. | "預防火災及疾病之威脅也" |
| NPWK19480131 leaf n3 (local news page), British note | INFERRED: The note gives the steps: a notice to leave on 27 Nov 1947, a court hearing in Dec 1947, and the eviction and demolition of the huts on 5 Jan 1948 without incident. | "執行驅逐" |
| NPWK19480131 leaf n3 (local news page), British note | INFERRED: The note says that on 12 Jan 1948 the police met organised resistance with stones and bricks, fired warning shots, then used tear gas. Six people were injured: five lightly and one with a gunshot wound in the abdomen, now out of danger. | "五名傷勢輕微" |
| NPWK19480131 leaf n3 (local news page), British note | The note says that the police were attacked outside the so-called Kowloon City, by people both inside and outside the walls. | "有在城內，亦有在城外" |
| NPWK19480131 leaf n3 (local news page), British note | INFERRED: The note names the two men arrested for inciting resistance: 朱沛唐, chairman of the residents association, and 劉毅夫, said to be a partner in a factory. On 13 Jan 1948 the Kowloon magistrate gave each three months' prison with hard labour. They did not in fact appeal. | "苦工三月" |
| NPWK19480131 leaf n3 (local news page), British note | INFERRED: The note blames the activity of the Bao'an county magistrate (寶安縣長), who spoke to the residents under the Chinese flag, for stirring up the trouble. | "寶安縣長" |
| NPWK19480131 leaf n3 (local news page), article 西鄉慰問團散賑龍城 | People of 西鄉 in Bao'an and its chamber of commerce collected more than 3,000 catties of rice from about 100 shops for the City residents. | "約有商店百餘間" |
| NPWK19480131 leaf n3 (local news page), same article | A group of eight brought the rice on 30 Jan 1948, but Customs at Man Kam To (文錦渡) stopped it, because the law did not allow food to leave China. They sold the rice in Shenzhen, brought the cash and bought rice in Hong Kong for the residents association. | "海關以糧食出口為法令所不許，不准放行" |
| NPWK19480131 leaf n3 (local news page), same article | The rice came to about 3,200 catties and was to be given out to the residents on 31 Jan 1948. | "查該批白米共三千二百餘斤" |

- **Leads:**
  - The English original of the Foreign Office reply to the Chinese Ambassador on Kowloon City (late Jan 1948), released in Hong Kong by the Government Information Services on 30 Jan 1948
  - The note of the Chinese Ambassador to the Foreign Office of 13 Jan 1948 on the Kowloon City evictions
  - Kowloon Magistracy case of 朱沛唐 and 劉毅夫, conviction of 13 Jan 1948
### china-3: 九龙城寨居民联合反对拆迁委员会发表声明 香港英当局应即撤销拆迁九龙城寨计划

- **Status:** Read on 2026-11-18; a checker checked a sample of the facts on 2026-11-20.
- **Citation:** Xinhua News Agency (新華社), Hong Kong, 19 Jan 1963: 「九龙城寨居民联合反对拆迁委员会发表声明 香港英当局应即撤销拆迁九龙城寨计划」, People's Daily (人民日報), 20 Jan 1963, p. 1. Read on the 老資料網 mirror: https://www.laoziliao.net/rmrb/1963-01-20-1
- **Class:** primary (A Xinhua news report of a press conference of the day before, with quotations from the residents' committee statement. A record made at the time.)
- **Date of the record:** 1963-01-19 (Xinhua dispatch on the press conference of 18 Jan 1963); printed 1963-01-20

| Page | Fact | Words in the source |
|---|---|---|
| People's Daily (人民日報), 20 Jan 1963, page 1 (第1版) | On 18 Jan 1963 the Anti-Demolition and Anti-Resettlement Committee of the Residents of Kowloon (九龍城寨居民聯合反對拆遷委員會; English name as in Peking Review, china-4) held a press conference in Hong Kong. | "九龙城寨居民联合反对拆迁委员会十八日在这里举行记者招待会" (The committee held a press conference here on the 18th.) |
| People's Daily (人民日報), 20 Jan 1963, page 1 (第1版) | More than 30 reporters of Chinese and English papers came, with about 40 resident representatives. | "到会的有香港中英文报纸记者三十余人，另有城寨居民代表约四十人" (More than thirty reporters of Hong Kong Chinese and English papers attended, with about forty City resident representatives.) |
| People's Daily (人民日報), 20 Jan 1963, page 1 (第1版) | The chairman of the committee, 黃中平, read the committee's written statement against the demolition. | "反对拆迁委员会主任委员黄中平在招待会上代表这个委员会发表了反对拆迁的书面声明" (The committee chairman 黃中平 read a written statement against the demolition on behalf of the committee.) |
| People's Daily (人民日報), 20 Jan 1963, page 1 (第1版) | The statement said that for some days the Hong Kong police had sent more officers to patrol day and night, and had threatened the resident representatives not to oppose the clearance. | "连日来香港警务处增派警员日夜进行巡逻，并恐吓威胁居民代表不准反对拆迁" (For days the police have sent more officers to patrol day and night, and have threatened resident representatives not to oppose the demolition.) |
| People's Daily (人民日報), 20 Jan 1963, page 1 (第1版) | The statement asked the Hong Kong authorities to respect the City as part of Chinese territory and the 'traditional living rights' of the residents. | "尊重城寨是中国领土的一部分和中国的主权地位以及居民的传统生活权利" (respect that the City is part of Chinese territory, China's sovereign status, and the residents' traditional living rights) |
| People's Daily (人民日報), 20 Jan 1963, page 1 (第1版) | The residents protested at once when the clearance attempt started in 1962 ('last year'). | "自去年香港英国当局企图拆迁九龙城寨以来，城寨居民立即提出抗议" (Since the British Hong Kong authorities attempted to clear the City last year, the residents protested at once.) |

- **Leads:**
  - Written statement of the Anti-Demolition and Anti-Resettlement Committee of the Residents of Kowloon (九龍城寨居民聯合反對拆遷委員會; English name as in Peking Review, china-4), read at the press conference of 18 Jan 1963 (quoted in part).
